### 🔴 Critical (10)

<a id="t-003"></a><a id="f-003"></a>
#### F-003 · SQL injection in login query bypasses authentication (routes/login.ts:34)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**Violates:** [`IV-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-004) (MUST) - Database queries MUST bind untrusted values through parameterized…

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-7](#tb-7) - auth-session → database · Query construction: Assumption 'credential lookups reach SQLite through Sequelize parameterized bindings with no raw SQL' is directly refuted by the raw template-literal query at `routes/login.ts:34`.

**Issue:** An attacker submits a crafted email value such as `' OR '1'='1'--` to POST `/rest/user/login`, causing the raw string-concatenated SQL query to return the first user row (typically admin) without a matching password, issuing a valid JWT for that account. Unauthenticated attacker authenticates as any user, including admin.

Full access to user data, order history, admin functions, and basket management for the impersonated account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 34 passes `req.body.email` and a hashed `req.body.password` directly into a template-literal SQL string via `models.sequelize.query()`. No parameterized binding is used for this credential lookup.

```typescript
// routes/login.ts:34

  return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    verifyPreLoginChallenges(req) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
    models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email || ''}' AND password = '${security.hash(req.body.password || '')}' AND deletedAt IS NULL`, { model: UserModel, plain: true }) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
      .then((authenticatedUser) => { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
        const user = utils.queryResultToJson(authenticatedUser)
        if (user.data?.id && user.data.totpSecret !== '') {
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-003](#m-003) — Use parameterized database queries (`login.ts:34`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.4](#34-sql-injection-in-login-query-bypasses-authentication)

<a id="t-004"></a><a id="f-004"></a>
#### F-004 · Hardcoded RSA private key enables arbitrary JWT forgery (lib/insecurity.ts:21)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**Violates:** [`DP-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/data-protection-cryptography#dp-005) (MUST) - Persistent credentials and cryptographic keys MUST come from external… · [`AC-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-005) (MUST) - Resource servers MUST validate every access `token` before use. JWT…

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → auth-session · Authentication: Assumption '`token` accepted only if signed with a key only the server holds' is violated: the private key is readable from committed source by any attacker.<br>[tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → backend-api · Authentication: Forged JWT crosses the external-to-backend trust boundary; `expressJwt` check at tb-1 is bypassed because the private signing key is exposed in source.

**Issue:** An attacker reads the RSA private key embedded in `lib/insecurity.ts` line 21, uses it offline to sign a JWT payload with any email, `role`, or `userId` claim, and submits the forged `token` to any authenticated API endpoint to impersonate an admin or arbitrary user. Complete authentication bypass for every account and `role`.

Attacker can forge tokens for admin, accounting, or any customer account, access all user data, trigger privileged operations, and render session revocation impossible.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 21 assigns the full RSA private key as a string literal in the module-level `privateKey` constant, which is then passed to `jwt.sign()` at line 54. The key is present in plaintext in the committed source.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ● [M-004](#m-004) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (`insecurity.ts:21`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.7](#37-hardcoded-rsa-private-key-enables-arbitrary-jwt-forgery)

<a id="t-005"></a><a id="f-005"></a>
#### F-005 · Insecure JWT Verification

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (4)

**Violates:** [`AC-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-005) (MUST) - Resource servers MUST validate every access `token` before use. JWT…

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → backend-api · Authentication: Algorithm confusion attack exploits the JWT validation leg of tb-1 where `token` integrity is verified before granting authenticated access.

**Instances (4):** 🟠 `lib/insecurity.ts:189`, 🟠 `lib/insecurity.ts:53`, 🟠 `lib/insecurity.ts:56`, 🔴 `routes/verify.ts:120`

**Issue:** Without an explicit algorithm allowlist, attackers can forge tokens with `alg:none` (older lib versions) or use the public key as an HMAC secret to mint valid signatures.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
// lib/insecurity.ts:189
export const updateAuthenticatedUsers = () => (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  const token = req.cookies.token || utils.jwtFrom(req)
  if (token && authenticatedUsers.get(token) === undefined) {
    jwt.verify(token, publicKey, (err: Error | null, decoded: any) => {
      if (err === null && decoded?.data !== undefined) {
        authenticatedUsers.put(token, decoded)
        res.cookie('token', token)
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ● [M-005](#m-005) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (`insecurity.ts:189`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.1](#31-insecure-jwt-verification-in-express-rest-api)

<a id="t-006"></a><a id="f-006"></a>
#### F-006 · SQL injection via email string interpolation in login (routes/login.ts:34)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**Violates:** [`IV-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-004) (MUST) - Database queries MUST bind untrusted values through parameterized… · [`IV-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-001) (MUST) - At each trust boundary, external input MUST be validated for expected…

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-6](#tb-6) - backend-api → database · Query construction: Injected SQL crosses the backend-api to database trust boundary; the tb-6 assumption of parameterized queries is explicitly violated at `routes/login.ts:34`.<br>[tb-7](#tb-7) - auth-session → database · Query construction: Trust boundary tb-7 assumes auth-session sends credential lookups via Sequelize parameterized bindings; `login.ts:34` proves that assumption violated for the login path.

**Issue:** An attacker sends POST `/rest/user/login` with email value `' OR 1=1--` and any password; `routes/login.ts:34` concatenates `req.body.email` directly into a raw SQL string via template literal, producing a query that returns all users; the application authenticates as the first returned row (typically the pre-seeded admin account), issuing a valid admin JWT without any valid credentials. Unauthenticated attacker gains admin-level JWT access, can exfiltrate all user records including hashed passwords, email addresses, and addresses, and perform any privileged operation including data export and order manipulation.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/login.ts:34` passes `req.body.email` through the template literal `SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email || ''}'...` to `models.sequelize.query` without parameterized binding; the Sequelize ORM is used for all other queries but bypassed here.

```typescript
// routes/login.ts:34

  return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    verifyPreLoginChallenges(req) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
    models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email || ''}' AND password = '${security.hash(req.body.password || '')}' AND deletedAt IS NULL`, { model: UserModel, plain: true }) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
      .then((authenticatedUser) => { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
        const user = utils.queryResultToJson(authenticatedUser)
        if (user.data?.id && user.data.totpSecret !== '') {
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-006](#m-006) — Use parameterized database queries (`login.ts:34`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-007"></a><a id="f-007"></a>
#### F-007 · SQL injection request data interpolated into a SQL string routes/search.ts:23

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**Violates:** [`IV-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-001) (MUST) - At each trust boundary, external input MUST be validated for expected… · [`IV-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-004) (MUST) - Database queries MUST bind untrusted values through parameterized…

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-6](#tb-6) - backend-api → database · Query construction: Trust boundary tb-6 assumes backend-api sends all queries via Sequelize parameterized bindings; `search.ts:23` proves that assumption is violated for the search route.

**Issue:** Interpolating request-controlled text into a SQL statement lets an attacker alter the query - exfiltrating or modifying arbitrary rows, bypassing authentication, or escalating to full database control.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
// routes/search.ts:23
  return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {

    criteria = (criteria.length <= 200) ? criteria : criteria.substring(0, 200)
    models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Products WHERE ((name LIKE '%${criteria}%' OR description LIKE '%${criteria}%') AND deletedAt IS NULL) ORDER BY name`) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line unionSqlInjectionChallenge dbSchemaChallenge
      .then(([products]: any) => {
        const dataString = JSON.stringify(products)
        if (challengeUtils.notSolved(challenges.unionSqlInjectionChallenge)) { // vuln-code-snippet hide-start
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-007](#m-007) — Use parameterized database queries (`search.ts:23`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.2](#32-sql-injection-request-data-interpolated-into-a-sql-string-in-express-rest-api)

<a id="t-008"></a><a id="f-008"></a>
#### F-008 · Insecure Direct Object Reference

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (20)

**Violates:** [`AC-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-002) (MUST) - Every protected operation MUST authorize the authenticated identity… · [`AC-006`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-006) (MUST) - Every request for a protected object MUST authorize the authenticated…

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - `Authorization` is implemented route by route

**Instances (20):** 🔴 `routes/address.ts:11`, 🔴 `routes/address.ts:18`, 🔴 `routes/address.ts:29`, 🟠 `routes/basketItems.ts:68`, 🔴 `routes/dataExport.ts:26`, 🟠 `routes/delivery.ts:34`, 🔴 `routes/deluxe.ts:25`, 🔴 `routes/deluxe.ts:30` … (+12 more)

**Issue:** Server-side authorization MUST derive the resource owner from the authenticated session (`req.user` / `req.session` / `req.auth`), never from attacker-controlled request data. Trusting `req.body.UserId` etc. enables horizontal privilege escalation across all authenticated tenants.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
// routes/address.ts:11

export function getAddress () {
  return async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
    const addresses = await AddressModel.findAll({ where: { UserId: req.body.UserId } })
    res.status(200).json({ status: 'success', data: addresses })
  }
}
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ● [M-008](#m-008) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (`address.ts:11`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-009"></a><a id="f-009"></a>
#### F-009 · Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Frontend SPA  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (8)

**Violates:** [`WEB-007`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/web-frontend-security#web-007) (MUST) - Untrusted data MUST be rendered with contextual output encoding or…

**Instances (8):** 🔴 `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`, 🔴 `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`, 🔴 `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`, 🔴 `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:91`, 🟢 `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`, 🟢 `frontend/src/app/track-result/track-result.component.ts:48`, 🟠 `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`, 🟢 `frontend/src/app/data-export/data-export.component.ts:57`

**Issue:** `filterTable()` reads the ?q= URL query parameter and at line 143 passes it directly to `this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam)`, suppressing Angular's XSS sanitization. An attacker delivers a crafted URL with a script payload in ?q= to a victim; when the victim navigates to it, Angular renders the payload as trusted HTML and the injected script reads the JWT from `localStorage`, exfiltrating it to an attacker-controlled endpoint.

Any authenticated user's JWT is exfiltrated in a single page load from a crafted URL - no prior access required. With the JWT stored in `localStorage`, the attacker gains full API access as the victim.

Impact is Critical given confirmed `localStorage` JWT storage.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `search-result.component.ts:143` calls `bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam)` where `queryParam` is read directly from `route.snapshot.queryParams.q` at line 136; recon subcategory dom-xss-source-sink-candidate confirms source-to-sink chain; known-threat record identifies this as the `localXssChallenge` vuln-line.

```typescript
// frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143
        this.io.socket().emit('verifyLocalXssChallenge', queryParam)
      }) // vuln-code-snippet hide-end
      this.dataSource.filter = queryParam.toLowerCase()
      this.searchValue = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line localXssChallenge xssBonusChallenge
      if (this.gridDataSourceSubscription) {
        this.gridDataSourceSubscription.unsubscribe()
      }
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ● [M-009](#m-009) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`search-result.component.ts:143`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.5](#35-cross-site-scripting-xss-in-search-result)

<a id="t-010"></a><a id="f-010"></a>
#### F-010 · JWT Bearer Token Stored in localStorage (login.component.ts:101)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Frontend SPA  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Violates:** [`WEB-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/web-frontend-security#web-002) (MUST) - Authentication tokens, secrets, and sensitive personal data MUST NOT be… · [`DP-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/data-protection-cryptography#dp-003) (MUST) - Credentials, bearer tokens, one-time codes, and other sensitive values…

**Instances (2):** 🔴 `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`, 🟠 `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:120`

**Issue:** Writes the JWT bearer `token` to `localStorage` after successful authentication. `localStorage` is readable by any JavaScript executing in the same origin.

The application has nine confirmed `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` XSS sinks; any one of them enables an attacker to read `localStorage.getItem('token')` and exfiltrate the JWT, achieving full account takeover without the user's credentials. Every authenticated user's JWT is extractable via XSS.

Combined with the eight other confirmed XSS sinks in the same SPA origin, this creates a high-probability, low-effort full account-takeover chain. Impact is Critical.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:101` stores the JWT in `localStorage`; `app.guard.ts:18` reads it back; `basket.service.ts:64` and `request.interceptor.ts:13` use it for authenticated API calls - all confirmed present; known-threat record explicitly identifies this pattern.

**Fix:** ● [M-010](#m-010) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies (`login.component.ts:101`)

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.6](#36-jwt-bearer-token-stored-in-localstorage)

<a id="t-011"></a><a id="f-011"></a>
#### F-011 · Mass assignment privileged field accepted from request body routes/verify.ts:53

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `routes/verify.ts:53`

**Violates:** [`SD-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/secure-design-business-logic#sd-002) (MUST) - Security-sensitive business rules, including ownership, transaction… · [`IV-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-001) (MUST) - At each trust boundary, external input MUST be validated for expected…

**Issue:** Server code that consumes `req.body.role` / `req.body.isAdmin` / etc. without an explicit allowlist trusts the client to behave. An attacker simply adds `{"role":"admin"}` to their request to escalate.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
// routes/verify.ts:53

export const registerAdminChallenge = () => (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.registerAdminChallenge, () => {
    return req.body && req.body.role === security.roles.admin
  })
  next()
}
```

**Fix:** ● [M-011](#m-011) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (`verify.ts:53`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.3](#33-mass-assignment-privileged-field-accepted-from-request-body)

<a id="t-012"></a><a id="f-012"></a>
#### F-012 · Derived private key enables unauthorized on-chain asset control

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:11`

**Violates:** [`DP-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/data-protection-cryptography#dp-005) (MUST) - Persistent credentials and cryptographic keys MUST come from external… · [`SD-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/secure-design-business-logic#sd-002) (MUST) - Security-sensitive business rules, including ownership, transaction…

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker reads the hardcoded mnemonic from `routes/checkKeys.ts` line 10, derives the HD wallet private key by calling `HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase(mnemonic)` locally, imports the private key into any Ethereum wallet client, and signs transactions to transfer on-chain assets held by the wallet address; blockchain transactions are irreversible and the application has no mechanism to detect or block this. An attacker who derives the private key gains complete signing authority over the Ethereum wallet: they can transfer any on-chain assets, mint or transfer NFTs attributed to the wallet, and perform any blockchain action as the wallet owner; these actions are irreversible.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `checkKeys.ts` line 11 calls `HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase(mnemonic)` where mnemonic is the hardcoded literal at line 10; line 12 extracts and uses `privateKey`, confirming the key derivation path is operational and the wallet holds or can hold real on-chain assets.

```typescript
// routes/checkKeys.ts:11
    try {
      const { HDNodeWallet } = await import('ethers')
      const mnemonic = 'purpose betray marriage blame crunch monitor spin slide donate sport lift clutch'
      const mnemonicWallet = HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase(mnemonic)
      const privateKey = mnemonicWallet.privateKey
      const publicKey = mnemonicWallet.publicKey
      const address = mnemonicWallet.address
```

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ● [M-012](#m-012) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (`checkKeys.ts:11`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

### 🟠 High (29)

<a id="t-001"></a><a id="f-001"></a>
#### F-001 · Client-Side-Only Admin Authorization Guard (app.guard.ts:52)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Frontend SPA  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:52`

**Violates:** [`AC-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-002) (MUST) - Every protected operation MUST authorize the authenticated identity…

**Issue:** `AdminGuard.canActivate()` at `app.guard.ts:52-58` reads the JWT from `localStorage`, decodes it client-side, and checks `payload.data.role` === `roles.admin`. This guard controls only Angular's client-side routing - it does not protect the backend API.

A non-admin user can bypass admin route restrictions entirely by calling admin-only API endpoints directly (`/rest/user/find`, etc.), or by manipulating their `localStorage` `token` to contain a forged admin `role` claim, since the SPA does not validate the JWT signature. If backend endpoints serving admin functionality do not independently enforce `role` authorization (a separate backend finding), the client-side guard provides no security barrier.

A sophisticated attacker can forge the `role` claim or make direct API calls, gaining unrestricted admin access.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `app.guard.ts:52-58` decodes the JWT client-side and checks the `role` claim without verifying the signature; `app.guard.ts:17-18` confirms login check reads only from `localStorage`; recon subcategory guard-without-server-authority-candidate at `app.guard.ts:6` confirms this pattern.

**Fix:** ◕ [M-001](#m-001) — Enforce authorization on the server (`app.guard.ts:52`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

**Violates:** [`AC-008`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-008) (MUST) - Authentication and recovery flows MUST issue one-time codes only…

<a id="t-014"></a><a id="f-014"></a>
#### F-014 · Open redirect via substring allowlist bypass (routes/redirect.ts:16)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/redirect.ts:16`

**Violates:** [`IV-007`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-007) (MUST) - When untrusted input influences an outbound destination, the…

**Issue:** An attacker crafts a URL such as `https://attacker.example.com/?q=https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop` and passes it as the to query parameter; `lib/insecurity.ts` `isRedirectAllowed` uses `url.includes(allowedUrl)` so the crafted URL contains an allowlisted string and passes validation, causing `res.redirect(toUrl)` to send the victim's browser to the attacker-controlled site. Victims following application-generated redirect links are transparently forwarded to attacker-controlled sites that can harvest credentials or session tokens under the application's trusted domain reputation.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `redirect.ts` line 16 calls `security.isRedirectAllowed(toUrl)` which at `lib/insecurity.ts` line 136 checks `url.includes(allowedUrl)` instead of an exact match; this permits any URL that embeds an allowlisted domain as a substring.

```typescript
// routes/redirect.ts:16
  return ({ query }: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    const toUrl: string = query.to as string
    if (security.isRedirectAllowed(toUrl)) {
      challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.redirectCryptoCurrencyChallenge, () => { return toUrl === 'https://explorer.dash.org/address/Xr556RzuwX6hg5EGpkybbv5RanJoZN17kW' || toUrl === 'https://blockchain.info/address/1AbKfgvw9psQ41NbLi8kufDQTezwG8DRZm' || toUrl === 'https://etherscan.io/address/0x0f933ab9fcaaa782d0279c300d73750e1311eae6' })
      challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.redirectChallenge, () => { return isUnintendedRedirect(toUrl) })
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-014](#m-014) — Validate redirect targets against an allowlist (`redirect.ts:16`)

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-015"></a><a id="f-015"></a>
#### F-015 · Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Socket\.IO Real-Time Service  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (4)

**Violates:** [`AC-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-002) (MUST) - Every protected operation MUST authorize the authenticated identity…

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-3](#tb-3) 🌐 External - external → websocket-rt · Authentication: External clients cross this boundary into the Socket\.IO endpoint; the authentication `assumption_leg` is violated because no identity check occurs on the handshake.

**Instances (4):** 🟡 `frontend/src/app/Services/socket-io.service.ts:22`, 🟠 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:23`, 🟠 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:33`, 🟠 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:40`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker establishes a Socket\.IO connection on port 3000 and receives all subsequent challenge-solved broadcasts - including CTF flag values and challenge descriptions - without presenting any identity `token`, because the connection handler at line 23 accepts every caller unconditionally. Any internet client can connect and receive real-time challenge-solved events, CTF flags, and challenge descriptions without being an authenticated user of the application, undermining confidentiality of all game state broadcast on this channel.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `io.on('connection', (socket: any) => { ... })` at line 23 executes for every connecting client with no preceding middleware, JWT check, or session validation. The controls-context explicitly confirms 'No per-connection authentication or identity verification on Socket\.IO handshake'.

```typescript
// lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:23
  globalWithSocketIO.io = io

  io.on('connection', (socket: any) => {
    if (firstConnectedSocket === null) {
      socket.emit('server started')
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-015](#m-015) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:23`)

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-016"></a><a id="f-016"></a>
#### F-016 · MD5 used for password hashing allows rapid offline cracking (insecurity.ts:41)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:41`

**Violates:** [`DP-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/data-protection-cryptography#dp-004) (MUST) - When local passwords are required, the application MUST reject inputs…

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** An attacker who obtains a copy of the Users table (via SQL injection, backup exposure, or insider access) uses GPU-accelerated `MD5` rainbow tables to reverse all stored password hashes within hours, recovering plaintext credentials for every account. All stored user passwords recoverable offline without any interaction with the running service.

Recovered passwords enable account takeover across the application and any external service where users reuse passwords.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 41 exports `hash = (data: string) => crypto.createHash('md5').update(data).digest('hex')`. This function is used in `routes/login.ts:34` to `hash` the submitted password before the SQL comparison, confirming `MD5` is the only password storage algorithm.

```typescript
// lib/insecurity.ts:41
}

export const hash = (data: string) => crypto.createHash('md5').update(data).digest('hex')
export const hmac = (data: string) => crypto.createHmac('sha256', 'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj').update(data).digest('hex')

```

**Fix:** Replace the broken `hash` with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-016](#m-016) — Use modern cryptographic hash and KDF algorithms (`insecurity.ts:41`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-328](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/328.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-017"></a><a id="f-017"></a>
#### F-017 · Password change endpoint uses HTTP GET enabling CSRF (changePassword.ts:13)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:13`

**Violates:** [`WEB-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/web-frontend-security#web-001) (MUST) - Applications that use cookie-based or other ambient browser credentials… · [`AC-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-003) (MUST) - Login, registration, credential recovery, verification, `token` issuance…

**Issue:** An attacker hosts a page containing `<img src='https://target/rest/user/change-password?new=hacked123&repeat=hacked123'>`. When a logged-in victim loads this page, the browser automatically issues the GET request with the victim's session cookie, changing their password to the attacker-chosen value.

An authenticated user's password can be changed to an attacker-controlled value via a single cross-site request, resulting in account takeover without the victim's knowledge or interaction beyond page load.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts` registers the handler as `app.get('/rest/user/change-password', ...)`. Inside the handler, `routes/changePassword.ts:13` reads `query.current`, `query.new`, and `query.repeat` from URL query parameters. No CSRF `token` or `SameSite` cookie enforcement is present. The cookie set at `lib/insecurity.ts:192` carries no `SameSite` attribute.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF `token` on every state-changing endpoint → ◕ [M-017](#m-017) — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests (`changePassword.ts:13`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

**Violates:** [`IV-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-005) (MUST) - File uploads MUST enforce size and count limits, verify content… · [`IV-008`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-008) (MUST) - Untrusted values MUST NOT select filesystem paths, commands…

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

**Issue:** A request-controlled path with `../` can read arbitrary files (`/etc/passwd`, source, secrets) or write outside the intended root.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript

        const filePath: string = path.resolve(req.body.layout).toLowerCase()
        const isForbiddenFile: boolean = (filePath.includes('ftp') || filePath.includes('ctf.key') || filePath.includes('encryptionkeys'))
        if (!isForbiddenFile) {
```

**Fix:** Resolve and normalise every constructed path and reject anything that escapes the intended base directory → ◕ [M-018](#m-018) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory (`dataErasure.ts:104`)

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-019"></a><a id="f-019"></a>
#### F-019 · ZIP Slip path traversal in complaint file upload (routes/fileUpload.ts:33)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:33`

**Violates:** [`IV-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-005) (MUST) - File uploads MUST enforce size and count limits, verify content… · [`IV-008`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-008) (MUST) - Untrusted values MUST NOT select filesystem paths, commands…

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → backend-api · Validation: Attacker-controlled ZIP entry path crosses the external-to-backend boundary and escapes the intended upload directory containment enforced at the validation leg.

**Issue:** An attacker uploads a ZIP file to POST `/file-upload` containing an entry with path ..`/ftp/legal.md`; `routes/fileUpload.ts:33` resolves the absolute path to verify containment but then writes the stream at the raw concatenated path 'uploads/complaints/' + `fileName` at line 34, allowing the attacker to overwrite `ftp/legal.md` or other server-side files outside the intended complaints directory without authentication. Attacker can overwrite arbitrary server-accessible files including `ftp/legal.md`; if writable paths include scripts or configuration files, this enables server-side code injection or application configuration tampering.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/fileUpload.ts:31-34`: `entry.path` is used as-is as `fileName`; `absolutePath` is computed via `path.resolve('uploads/complaints/' + fileName)` for the containment check at line 33; but the actual write pipeline at line 34 uses `fs.createWriteStream('uploads/complaints/' + fileName)`, which resolves differently when `fileName` contains `../`, enabling traversal to ftp/ and other server-side directories.

```typescript
// routes/fileUpload.ts:33
    const absolutePath = path.resolve('uploads/complaints/' + fileName)
    challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.fileWriteChallenge, () => { return absolutePath === path.resolve('ftp/legal.md') })
    if (absolutePath.includes(path.resolve('.'))) {
      await pipeline(entry.stream(), fs.createWriteStream('uploads/complaints/' + fileName))
    }
```

**Fix:** Resolve and normalise every constructed path and reject anything that escapes the intended base directory → ◕ [M-019](#m-019) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory (`fileUpload.ts:33`)

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-020"></a><a id="f-020"></a>
#### F-020 · LLM01 Prompt injection via unfiltered user messages (routes/chat.ts:191)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:191`

**Violates:** [`LLM-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/ai-llm-security#llm-001) (MUST) - Prompts, retrieved documents, files, tool results, memory, and model…

**Issue:** LLM01 Prompt Injection: an attacker sends POST `/rest/chat` with a messages array containing adversarially crafted role-override entries (e.g., a fake system message instructing the model to generate a 100% discount coupon); `routes/chat.ts:191` assigns `req.body?.messages` directly to messages without `role` filtering and passes the full array to `streamText` at line 207, allowing the attacker to override the operator system prompt and trigger `generateCoupon` for arbitrary discount amounts. Attacker can override operator instructions to generate arbitrary discount coupons, extract the confidential 15% escalation policy from the system prompt, manipulate product search results, or cause the model to return incorrect order information to legitimate users.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:191` assigns `req.body?.messages` ?? [] without validation; line 207 passes messages directly to `streamText` as the conversation history, which is combined with the system prompt; no `role` allowlisting, message count limit, or injection filtering is applied before the LLM call.

```typescript
// routes/chat.ts:191

    const model = config.get<string>('application.chatBot.model')
    const messages = req.body?.messages ?? []
    const userName = await getUserNameFromToken(req)

```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-020](#m-020) — Filter and validate user message roles before passing to streamText in the chat… (`chat.ts:191`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-74](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/74.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-021"></a><a id="f-021"></a>
#### F-021 · Non-cryptographic RNG for a secret/token lib/insecurity.ts:53

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:53`

**Violates:** [`DP-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/data-protection-cryptography#dp-002) (MUST) - Session, reset, activation, and other bearer tokens MUST be generated…

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** A predictable token/secret lets an attacker guess or brute-force session identifiers, reset links, or OTPs.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
// lib/insecurity.ts:53

export const isAuthorized = () => expressJwt(({ secret: publicKey }) as any)
export const denyAll = () => expressJwt({ secret: '' + Math.random() } as any)
export const authorize = (user = {}) => jwt.sign(user, privateKey, { expiresIn: '6h', algorithm: 'RS256' })
export const verify = (token: string) => token ? (jws.verify as ((token: string, secret: string) => boolean))(token, publicKey) : false
```

**Fix:** Switch to a cryptographically secure RNG (`crypto.randomBytes` / OS `/dev/urandom`) → ◕ [M-021](#m-021) — Use cryptographically secure random values (`insecurity.ts:53`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-330](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/330.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-022"></a><a id="f-022"></a>
#### F-022 · NoSQL \$where JS injection in chat product reviews tool (routes/chat.ts:149)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:149`

**Violates:** [`IV-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-004) (MUST) - Database queries MUST bind untrusted values through parameterized… · [`IV-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-003) (MUST) - Untrusted data MUST use a safe, data-only parser with an explicit… · [`LLM-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/ai-llm-security#llm-005) (MUST) - Model output MUST be validated against a bounded schema and encoded for…

**Issue:** The `getProductReviews` LLM tool concatenates a product ID directly into a MongoDB \$where JavaScript expression string; while `Number(id)` coercion limits direct string injection, the \$where operator executes a server-side JavaScript function per document, enabling an attacker who manipulates the LLM output or submits crafted tool input to run arbitrary JavaScript in the MongoDB context, such as `sleep()` for DoS or conditional extraction for data exfiltration. MongoDB server-side JavaScript execution via \$where enables collection-wide table scan, potential data exfiltration beyond the target product review, and DoS via slow JavaScript expressions executed per document.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:149`: `db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + productId })` where `productId` is `Number(id)` derived from `z.string()` LLM tool input; MongoDB \$where executes a JS function per document with no query optimizer index; direct string injection is limited by `Number()` but the \$where operator itself introduces server-side JavaScript execution risk.

```typescript
// routes/chat.ts:149
        execute: async ({ id }) => {
          const productId = Number(id)
          return await db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + productId }) as Review[]
        }
      }),
```

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◕ [M-022](#m-022) — Use parameterized database queries (`chat.ts:149`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-023"></a><a id="f-023"></a>
#### F-023 · JWT returned in JSON response body stored in localStorage (routes/login.ts:26)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:26`

**Violates:** [`WEB-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/web-frontend-security#web-002) (MUST) - Authentication tokens, secrets, and sensitive personal data MUST NOT be…

**Issue:** A successful login at POST `/rest/user/login` returns the bearer `token` in the JSON response body (`authentication.token`). The SPA stores it in `localStorage`.

Any XSS payload executing in the application origin reads `localStorage.getItem('token')` and exfiltrates the `token` to an attacker-controlled server, providing a full 6-hour session. A stored or reflected XSS vulnerability anywhere in the application origin can steal the bearer `token`, granting the attacker an authenticated session valid for 6 hours with no server-side revocation possible before expiry.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/login.ts:26` executes `res.json({ authentication: { token, bid: basket.id, umail: user.email } })`, returning the JWT in the response body rather than setting an HttpOnly cookie. Architecture confirms the frontend SPA consumes tokens from `localStorage`.

```typescript
// routes/login.ts:26
        const token = security.authorize(authenticatedUser)
        security.authenticatedUsers.put(token, authenticatedUser)
        res.json({ authentication: { token, bid: basket.id, umail: user.email } })
      }).catch((error: Error) => {
        next(error)
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-023](#m-023) — Deliver JWT via HttpOnly SameSite=Lax cookie instead of response body (`login.ts:26`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-522](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/522.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-024"></a><a id="f-024"></a>
#### F-024 · Passwords transmitted as GET query parameters expose credentials

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:14`

**Violates:** [`DP-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/data-protection-cryptography#dp-003) (MUST) - Credentials, bearer tokens, one-time codes, and other sensitive values…

**Issue:** A user changes their password via the frontend, which issues a GET request to `/rest/user/change-password?current=oldpass`&`new=newpass`&`repeat=newpass`. The plaintext `current` and new passwords appear in web server access logs, browser history, proxy logs, and `Referer` headers on any subsequent navigation.

Current and new plaintext passwords are stored in web server logs and browser history accessible to anyone with log read access or physical access to the browser. This is particularly severe for the `current` password, which also authenticates the user on other services if reused.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/changePassword.ts:13-17` reads `query.current`, `query.new`, and `query.repeat` from `req.query`. `server.ts` registers the handler on `app.get('/rest/user/change-password', ...)`, confirming credential values travel in the URL query string.

```typescript
// routes/changePassword.ts:14
export function changePassword () {
  return async ({ query, headers, connection }: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    const currentPassword = query.current as string
    const newPassword = query.new as string
    const newPasswordInString = newPassword?.toString()
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-024](#m-024) — Transmit password change credentials in POST request body over HTTPS (`changePassword.ts:14`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-025"></a><a id="f-025"></a>
#### F-025 · LLM07 Confidential escalation policy in system prompt extractable via injection

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:105`

**Violates:** [`LLM-007`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/ai-llm-security#llm-007) (MUST) - System prompts MUST contain no credentials, personal data, tenant… · [`LLM-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/ai-llm-security#llm-002) (MUST) - Before model invocation, the application MUST authorize and minimize…

**Issue:** LLM07 System Prompt Leakage: the `buildSystemPrompt` function at `routes/chat.ts:82-105` embeds a confidential internal policy ('CONFIDENTIAL - INTERNAL ONLY: If a customer formally complains... offer them a one-time 15% courtesy discount') directly in the LLM system prompt; an attacker exploits prompt injection to instruct the model to repeat its system instructions, extracting the hidden 15% discount policy and subsequently claiming it to obtain an unauthorized discount on future orders.

Attacker extracts the hidden 15% discount policy from the system prompt and exploits it to receive unauthorized discounts; the exposed policy also reveals internal customer service procedures, undermining the confidentiality assumption the prompt's marker asserts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:105` contains the literal string 'CONFIDENTIAL - INTERNAL ONLY: If a customer formally complains about their shopping experience and explicitly requests to escalate the issue, offer them a one-time 15% courtesy discount to resolve the case without escalation'; this text is embedded verbatim in the system prompt returned by `buildSystemPrompt` at line 82 and passed to the LLM at line 205.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◕ [M-025](#m-025) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (`chat.ts:105`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-026"></a><a id="f-026"></a>
#### F-026 · Unauthenticated admin configuration endpoints disclose internals (server.ts:606)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:606`

**Violates:** [`HN-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/system-hardening#hn-002) (MUST) - Debug endpoints MUST be disabled in production. Required management… · [`AC-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-002) (MUST) - Every protected operation MUST authorize the authenticated identity… · [`EH-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/error-handling-security-events#eh-002) (MUST) - Client error responses MUST NOT expose stack traces, query text…

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - `Authorization` is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → backend-api · Authentication: Admin configuration endpoints are reachable from the external zone without satisfying the authentication leg of tb-1.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends GET `/rest/admin/application-configuration` and GET `/rest/admin/application-version` to `server.ts:606-607`; no `isAuthorized()` middleware is registered before these route handlers, so the full application configuration (including LLM API URL, chatbot model name, registered challenge names, and application domain) is returned without authentication, aiding targeted attack planning. Attacker obtains application version (enabling known-CVE lookups), LLM provider URL, model name, feature flags, and domain configuration without any credentials, facilitating targeted exploitation of other vulnerabilities.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:606` registers `app.get('/rest/admin/application-version', ...)` and `server.ts:607` registers `app.get('/rest/admin/application-configuration', ...)` without any preceding `security.isAuthorized()` or `security.isAccounting()` middleware call; no authentication middleware appears in the route chain for either endpoint.

```typescript
// server.ts:606
  app.post('/rest/basket/:id/checkout', placeOrder())
  app.put('/rest/basket/:id/coupon/:coupon', utils.asyncHandler(applyCoupon()))
  app.get('/rest/admin/application-version', utils.asyncHandler(retrieveAppVersion()))
  app.get('/rest/admin/application-configuration', utils.asyncHandler(retrieveAppConfiguration()))
  app.get('/rest/repeat-notification', utils.asyncHandler(repeatNotification()))
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-026](#m-026) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (`server.ts:606`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-027"></a><a id="f-027"></a>
#### F-027 · Payment card numbers stored as unencrypted INTEGER (models/card.ts:39)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - SQLite Data Store (Sequelize ORM)  ·  **Location:** `models/card.ts:39`

**Violates:** [`DP-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/data-protection-cryptography#dp-001) (MUST) - Confidential data at rest MUST be protected with an…

**Issue:** An attacker who executes a UNION-injection SQL query (🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — SQL injection request data interpolated into a SQL string `routes/search.ts:23`) or gains direct read access to the SQLite file reads the Cards table and retrieves full 16-digit card numbers (`cardNum`), cardholder names, expiry month, and expiry year for all users in plaintext, with no encryption applied at rest. Full payment card numbers (PAN), cardholder names, and expiry data are readable by any attacker who reaches the SQLite file or exploits the SQL injection path, violating PCI-DSS requirements and exposing users to financial fraud.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `models/card.ts:39` defines `cardNum` as `DataTypes.INTEGER` with range validation (1000000000000000 to 9999999999999998) but no encryption transform in a setter or before-save hook. The control inventory contains no entry for card-at-rest encryption. Sequelize stores the integer value directly in the SQLite file.

```typescript
// models/card.ts:39
      fullName: DataTypes.STRING,
      cardNum: {
        type: DataTypes.INTEGER,
        validate: {
          isInt: true,
```

**Fix:** Encrypt the data in transit and at rest with vetted primitives → ◕ [M-027](#m-027) — Encrypt card numbers at rest with a server-side key before SQLite storage (`card.ts:39`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-028"></a><a id="f-028"></a>
#### F-028 · Hardcoded wallet mnemonic exposes private key material (routes/checkKeys.ts:10)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**Violates:** [`DP-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/data-protection-cryptography#dp-005) (MUST) - Persistent credentials and cryptographic keys MUST come from external…

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → backend-api · Authentication: The authentication leg of tb-1 assumes wallet ownership verification is cryptographically sound; the hardcoded mnemonic defeats this assumption since any party can derive the private key and impersonate the wallet.

**Issue:** Any party with read access to the repository can read the mnemonic 'purpose betray marriage blame crunch monitor spin slide donate sport lift clutch' line 10, derive the corresponding HD wallet private key using `ethers.HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase()`, and obtain the full private key without any server interaction or authentication. The private key of an active Ethereum wallet is disclosed to any repository reader; given the architecture assumption that 'on-chain key material is confidential', its exposure invalidates the wallet's security boundary and enables unauthorized signing of blockchain transactions.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `checkKeys.ts` line 10 hardcodes the mnemonic phrase as a string literal inside the route handler; the private key is derived at runtime from this literal at line 11 and compared against user-supplied input at line 16-18, confirming the credential is operationally active.

```typescript
// routes/checkKeys.ts:10
    try {
      const { HDNodeWallet } = await import('ethers')
      const mnemonic = 'purpose betray marriage blame crunch monitor spin slide donate sport lift clutch'
      const mnemonicWallet = HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase(mnemonic)
      const privateKey = mnemonicWallet.privateKey
```

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◕ [M-028](#m-028) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (`checkKeys.ts:10`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-029"></a><a id="f-029"></a>
#### F-029 · CTF flags broadcast to all unauthenticated sockets (lib/challengeUtils.ts:75)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Socket\.IO Real-Time Service  ·  **Location:** `lib/challengeUtils.ts:75`

**Violates:** [`AC-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-002) (MUST) - Every protected operation MUST authorize the authenticated identity…

**Issue:** An attacker with no account connects to the Socket\.IO server and immediately receives - via the existing notifications replay loop (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:29-31`) and via the real-time broadcast - the full challenge-solved notification object which includes the CTF flag field (set at `challengeUtils.ts:52`) for every challenge solved by any user. The attacker harvests these flags without performing any challenge.

CTF flag values, which are the secrets that participants are supposed to discover by solving challenges, are disclosed to any unauthenticated internet client that connects to the WebSocket endpoint. This completely undermines the CTF competition integrity and the confidentiality of the game progression data.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `challengeUtils.ts` line 52 computes `const flag = utils.ctfFlag(challenge.name)` and includes it in the notification object at lines 62–70. Line 75 calls `globalWithSocketIO.io.emit('challenge solved', notification)` which broadcasts to all connected sockets. `registerWebsocketEvents.ts` lines 29–31 replay all existing notifications (including flags) to every new connection. No authentication check exists at either emission point.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◕ [M-029](#m-029) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (`challengeUtils.ts:75`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-030"></a><a id="f-030"></a>
#### F-030 · No rate limiting on login endpoint enables credential stuffing and DoS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:32`

**Violates:** [`AC-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-003) (MUST) - Login, registration, credential recovery, verification, `token` issuance…

**Issue:** An attacker sends thousands of POST `/rest/user/login` requests per minute from one or more source addresses. The endpoint performs a Sequelize raw SQL query and an `MD5` `hash` computation per request; sustained flooding saturates the Node\.js I/O thread pool, degrading or halting service for legitimate users while simultaneously testing credential lists.

Service unavailability for legitimate users during flooding attacks and unrestricted credential stuffing against all registered accounts, with no account lockout or alert triggered.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A grep for rate-limit middleware in `routes/login.ts` returns zero hits. `server.ts` applies `rateLimit` only to `/rest/user/reset-password`. No per-IP or per-account throttle is registered on POST `/rest/user/login`, POST `/rest/2fa/verify`, or GET `/rest/user/change-password`.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◕ [M-030](#m-030) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`login.ts:32`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-031"></a><a id="f-031"></a>
#### F-031 · LLM10 No rate limiting on chat endpoint enables unbounded LLM cost

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:638`

**Violates:** [`LLM-010`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/ai-llm-security#llm-010) (MUST) - LLM features MUST bound input size, output tokens, requests per user… · [`AC-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-003) (MUST) - Login, registration, credential recovery, verification, `token` issuance…

**Issue:** LLM10 Unbounded Consumption: an unauthenticated attacker sends a high volume of concurrent POST `/rest/chat` requests with large messages arrays; `server.ts:638` registers the endpoint with no rate limiting middleware and no per-source request cap; each request triggers up to 10 LLM tool calls (`stopWhen: stepCountIs`(10)), consuming external LLM API `token` budget and exhausting the configured provider's rate limits, causing service degradation for all users. Attacker exhausts the external LLM provider's `token` budget, incurs unbounded API costs for the operator, and degrades or eliminates chat service availability for all legitimate users; no authentication is required to trigger the attack.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:638`: `app.post('/rest/chat', utils.asyncHandler(chat()))` has no `rateLimit` middleware; `routes/chat.ts:209` sets `stopWhen: stepCountIs`(10) but no per-user, per-IP, or global request rate cap is present; controls-context confirms no rate limiting is detected on any endpoint.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◕ [M-031](#m-031) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`server.ts:638`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-032"></a><a id="f-032"></a>
#### F-032 · No rate limiting on login endpoint enables credential stuffing (server.ts:596)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:596`

**Violates:** [`AC-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-003) (MUST) - Login, registration, credential recovery, verification, `token` issuance…

**Issue:** An attacker runs a credential stuffing or brute-force attack against POST `/rest/user/login`; `server.ts:596` registers the login endpoint without any `rateLimit` middleware; only `/rest/user/reset-password` at line 343 has a rate limit configured, leaving login unprotected; the attacker can send unlimited authentication attempts per second without triggering any throttling or lockout. Attacker can enumerate and crack user credentials via unlimited login attempts; combined with the `MD5` password hashing at `lib/insecurity.ts:41`, online brute force against weak passwords succeeds rapidly without any server-side friction.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:596`: `app.post('/rest/user/login', login())` is registered with no `rateLimit` middleware in the request chain; `server.ts:343-347` shows `rateLimit` is used only for `/rest/user/reset-password`; controls-context confirms no rate limiting detected on any endpoint other than reset-password.

```typescript
// server.ts:596

  /* Custom Restful API */
  app.post('/rest/user/login', login())
  app.get('/rest/user/change-password', utils.asyncHandler(changePassword()))
  app.post('/rest/user/reset-password', utils.asyncHandler(resetPassword()))
```

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◕ [M-032](#m-032) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (`server.ts:596`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-033"></a><a id="f-033"></a>
#### F-033 · Uncontrolled Socket\.IO connection intake (registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Socket\.IO Real-Time Service  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Violates:** [`AC-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-003) (MUST) - Login, registration, credential recovery, verification, `token` issuance… · [`IV-006`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-006) (MUST) - Input-driven work MUST enforce request and decompressed-size limits…

**Instances (2):** 🟡 `frontend/src/app/Services/socket-io.service.ts:22`, 🟠 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`

**Issue:** An attacker opens thousands of concurrent Socket\.IO connections to port 3000. Because the Server constructor at line 20 specifies no `maxConnections` limit and no connection-rate middleware is installed, the Node\.js event loop processes each connection callback and emits the full notifications array to every one.

The resulting memory and CPU exhaustion degrades or terminates the co-located REST API. An attacker can exhaust Node\.js heap and event-loop capacity by opening mass concurrent connections, causing the REST API (co-located in the same process) to stop responding for all legitimate users until the process is restarted.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - new `Server(server, { cors: { origin: 'http://localhost:4200' } })` at line 20 creates the Socket\.IO server with no `maxConnections`, no connection-rate limit, and no per-IP throttle. The `on('connection')` handler at line 23-52 iterates the entire notifications array for each connection (line 29-31) without any bound, amplifying the per-connection cost.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◕ [M-033](#m-033) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-034"></a><a id="f-034"></a>
#### F-034 · Password change succeeds without current password when field is omitted

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Authentication and Session Management  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**Issue:** An authenticated attacker or CSRF gadget sends GET `/rest/user/change-password?new=newpass`&`repeat=newpass` omitting the `current` parameter. The guard `if (currentPassword && security.hash(currentPassword) !== loggedInUser.data.password)` short-circuits because `currentPassword` is undefined/falsy, allowing the password update to proceed.

Combined with the CSRF vector (🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Password change endpoint uses HTTP GET enabling CSRF (`changePassword.ts:13`)), any attacker can lock out a victim from their own account. An authenticated user can change their own password without knowing the `current` one.

Chained with the CSRF vulnerability (🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Password change endpoint uses HTTP GET enabling CSRF (`changePassword.ts:13`)), an attacker can change a victim's password via a cross-site request, permanently locking the victim out.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/changePassword.ts:39` evaluates `if (currentPassword && ...)`: when `currentPassword` is absent from the query string, the condition is falsy and the password update at line 51 proceeds unconditionally. The `currentPassword` check is therefore not mandatory.

```typescript
// routes/changePassword.ts:39
    }

    if (currentPassword && security.hash(currentPassword) !== loggedInUser.data.password) {
      res.status(401).send(res.__('Current password is not correct.'))
      return
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-034](#m-034) — Require and enforce current password verification before accepting password cha… (`changePassword.ts:39`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-035"></a><a id="f-035"></a>
#### F-035 · Unauthenticated file upload endpoints allow anonymous content injection

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Express REST API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:309`

**Violates:** [`AC-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-002) (MUST) - Every protected operation MUST authorize the authenticated identity… · [`IV-005`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-005) (MUST) - File uploads MUST enforce size and count limits, verify content… · [`SD-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/secure-design-business-logic#sd-003) (MUST) - High-impact or irreversible operations MUST require an authenticated…

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → backend-api · Authentication: File upload endpoints are reachable from the external zone without satisfying the authentication leg of the tb-1 trust boundary.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends POST `/file-upload`, POST `/profile/image/file`, or POST `/profile/image/url` without any authentication `token`; `server.ts:309-311` registers all three endpoints without `security.isAuthorized()` middleware, allowing anonymous users to upload files, inject complaint content, and trigger server-side file processing (ZIP extraction, XML parsing, YAML parsing) without establishing any user identity. Any internet user can upload arbitrary files and trigger server-side processing (ZIP extraction with path traversal, XML parsing with XXE, YAML bomb) without authentication, expanding the attack surface and enabling the ZIP Slip and XXE vulnerabilities to be exploited anonymously.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:309`: `app.post('/file-upload', uploadToMemory.single('file'), ensureFileIsPassed, ...)` has no `isAuthorized()` call in the middleware chain; `server.ts:310-311` similarly omit `isAuthorized()` for profile image upload endpoints; known-threats-context confirms 'Unauthenticated file upload endpoints: POST `/file-upload`, POST `/profile/image/file`, POST `/profile/image/url`'.

```typescript
// server.ts:309
  app.use(bodyParser.urlencoded({ extended: true }))
  /* File Upload */
  app.post('/file-upload', uploadToMemory.single('file'), ensureFileIsPassed, metrics.observeFileUploadMetricsMiddleware(), checkUploadSize, checkFileType, handleZipFileUpload, handleXmlUpload, handleYamlUpload)
  app.post('/profile/image/file', uploadToMemory.single('file'), ensureFileIsPassed, metrics.observeFileUploadMetricsMiddleware(), utils.asyncHandler(profileImageFileUpload()))
  app.post('/profile/image/url', uploadToMemory.single('file'), utils.asyncHandler(profileImageUrlUpload()))
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-035](#m-035) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (`server.ts:309`)

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

**Violates:** [`AC-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-002) (MUST) - Every protected operation MUST authorize the authenticated identity… · [`LLM-006`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/ai-llm-security#llm-006) (MUST) - Each model-connected tool action MUST be authenticated and authorized… · [`SD-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/secure-design-business-logic#sd-003) (MUST) - High-impact or irreversible operations MUST require an authenticated…

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → backend-api · Authentication: The unauthenticated chat endpoint is reachable from the external zone without satisfying the authentication leg of the tb-1 trust boundary.

**Issue:** LLM06 Excessive Agency: an unauthenticated attacker sends POST `/rest/chat` without any authentication `token`; `server.ts:638` registers the endpoint with no `isAuthorized()` middleware; the attacker crafts a prompt that satisfies the system prompt's coupon policy conditions and causes the model to invoke the `generateCoupon` tool at `routes/chat.ts:181`, which generates a valid discount coupon code via `security.generateCoupon` without any user identity check or authorization. Unauthenticated attacker obtains valid discount coupon codes by social-engineering the LLM through the chat interface, causing direct financial loss from unauthorized discounts applied at checkout.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:638`: `app.post('/rest/chat', utils.asyncHandler(chat()))` has no `security.isAuthorized()` call; `routes/chat.ts:181-186`: the `generateCoupon` tool executes `security.generateCoupon(discount)` without checking whether a user is authenticated; `routes/chat.ts:42-46`: `getUserId` returns undefined for unauthenticated callers but this is only used by the `getOrderById` tool, not the `generateCoupon` tool.

```typescript

  /* Chat API endpoint */

  /* Web3 API endpoints */
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-036](#m-036) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (`server.ts:638`)
