**How:**

1. Add `express-rate-limit` middleware at `server.ts` before line 638 with `windowMs: 60000`, max: 20 per IP, using a Redis store for cross-instance enforcement.
2. For authenticated users, enforce a per-user daily `token` budget tracked in the database; reject requests that exceed the budget with HTTP 429.
3. Set explicit `max_tokens` limits in the `streamText` call at `routes/chat.ts:203` (e.g., `maxTokens: 1024`) to bound per-request cost.
4. Add an alerting rule that fires when total LLM input or output `token` counters (already instrumented via `prom-client`) exceed a configurable threshold within a time window.

<a id="m-032"></a>
#### M-032 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-032](#f-032) — No rate limiting on login endpoint enables credential stuffing (`server.ts:596`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html)

- [`AC-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-003) (MUST) - Login, registration, credential recovery, verification, `token` issuance…

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:596`

**How:**

1. Add `express-rate-limit` middleware at `server.ts` before line 596: `rateLimit({ windowMs: 15*60*1000, max: 10, keyGenerator: ({ headers, ip }) => headers['X-Forwarded-For'] ?? ip })`.
2. Add account-level lockout: after 5 failed attempts within 15 minutes, temporarily lock the account and require email verification to unlock.
3. Log each failed attempt with IP, attempted email, and timestamp (per [`LM-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/security-logging-monitoring#lm-001)) for monitoring and alerting.
4. Verify: send 15 POST `/rest/user/login` requests with wrong credentials and assert requests 11-15 return HTTP 429.

**Verification:** Send 15 failed login attempts for the same account in rapid succession and assert HTTP 429 is returned before the 15th attempt.

<a id="m-033"></a>
#### M-033 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — Uncontrolled Socket\.IO connection intake (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Requirements at stake:**

- [`AC-003`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-003) (MUST) - Login, registration, credential recovery, verification, `token` issuance…
- [`IV-006`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-006) (MUST) - Input-driven work MUST enforce request and decompressed-size limits…

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`

**How:**

1. Apply a Socket\.IO connection middleware that tracks per-source-IP connection count using an in-process sliding-window counter. Reject connections from a source that exceeds the configured threshold (e.g. 10 concurrent connections per IP) by calling `next(new Error('rate limit exceeded'))`. Following BP-API-HARDENING [§4](#4-assets), this control should be effective across processes if the application is clustered (use a Redis-backed rate-limit store such as rate-limiter-flexible).
2. Add a per-event rate limit inside the connection handler using a similar sliding-window counter keyed on `socket.id` to prevent event-flooding attacks from a single connection.
3. Add a load test asserting that 500 concurrent connections from the same IP are rejected after the threshold and that the REST API on the same port remains responsive during the connection flood.

**Verification:** Use a tool such as artillery or a simple Node\.js script to open 200 concurrent WebSocket connections from localhost; confirm connections beyond the limit are rejected with an error and that the `/rest/admin/application-version` REST endpoint remains responsive with sub-200ms latency.

**Blueprint:** [BP-API-VALIDATION](https://appsec.int.example.com/blueprints/api-validation) - Input Validation · [3. Boundary & Size Limits](https://appsec.int.example.com/blueprints/api-validation#_boundary_size_limits) (for [`IV-006`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/input-validation-data-handling#iv-006))

**Reference:** OWASP ASVS v4.0 §13.4 - WebSocket and Real-Time Communication Security

- 🟠 [F-039](#f-039) — CI/CD Workflow Token Permissions Not Restricted (`lint-fixer.yml:22`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html) - Improper Access Control

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml:22`

**How:**

1. Add the following permissions block immediately after the `on:` trigger in `lint-fixer.yml`: `permissions: contents: write` - this is the minimum scope required for the auto-commit step. All other permission scopes will default to `none`, eliminating write access to packages, actions, issues, and secrets.
2. Apply the same pattern to `ci.yml`, `rebase.yml`, and `release.yml: add` job-level `permissions:` blocks scoped to the minimum each job requires (e.g., release jobs need `contents: write` for release creation, the docker job needs no additional permissions beyond the default).
3. Add a CI check using `actionlint` or `zizmor` to detect future workflow files missing explicit permissions blocks.

_Example implementation in `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml:22`: it applies **Apply least-privilege filesystem access**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Centralize access decisions in a single middleware.
function requireRole(role: 'admin' | 'user') {
  return (req, res, next) =>
    req.user?.role === role ? next() : res.status(403).end()
}
```

**Verification:** After adding the permissions block, create a test PR and confirm in the Actions run summary that the `GITHUB_TOKEN` permissions panel shows only `contents: write` and all other scopes as `none`; verify that `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/permissions/workflow` returns `default_workflow_permissions: read`.

**Reference:** [GitHub Docs: Security Hardening For Github Actions](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-for-github-actions/security-guides/security-hardening-for-github-actions#restricting-permissions-for-tokens)

<a id="m-040"></a>
#### M-040 — Harden the authentication flow

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-040](#f-040) — Predictable OAuth-Derived Password (`oauth.component.ts:30`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Requirements at stake:**

- [`AC-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-004) (MUST) - Workforce and privileged users MUST authenticate through the…

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** High · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Remove the derived-password registration and login from `oauth.component.ts:30-46`; instead, the BFF should exchange the OAuth `token` for a server-issued session cookie directly, without creating a local password.
2. Migrate existing OAuth user accounts to invalidate the derived password and mark them as OAuth-only accounts with no local password.
3. Implement a backend check that rejects password-based login for accounts flagged as OAuth-only.
4. Write a test confirming that a locally computed btoa-derived password is rejected by `/rest/user/login` for any OAuth-registered account after migration.

**Verification:** Register via Google OAuth; compute `btoa(email.split('').reverse().join(''))` for the test account; POST it to `/rest/user/login` and confirm a 401 response.

Configure the Client in the Identity Provider](https://appsec.int.example.com/blueprints/api-authz-enduser#_sso_client_config) · [1. Implement a Backend-for-Frontend (BFF)](https://appsec.int.example.com/blueprints/api-authz-enduser#_bff_pattern) (for [`AC-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-004))

**Requirements at stake:**

- [`LM-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/security-logging-monitoring#lm-001) (MUST) - Applications MUST record security-relevant events including…
- [`LM-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/security-logging-monitoring#lm-002) (MUST) - Security events MUST use a structured, versioned format with a…

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:26`

**How:**

1. Import or instantiate the project's logger in `routes/login.ts` and emit a structured event on each authentication attempt: `logger.info({ event: 'AUTH_SUCCESS', userId: user.id, ip: req.ip, timestamp: new Date().toISOString() })` in `afterLogin`, and `logger.warn({ event: 'AUTH_FAILURE', email: req.body.email, ip: req.ip })` on line 50.
2. Ensure the logger is the same centralized logger used elsewhere in the application (consistent with [`LM-001`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/security-logging-monitoring#lm-001)`/LM-002` requirements), not a new ad-hoc one.
3. Confirm sensitive values (password, `token`) are never included in the log entry.
4. Add a test asserting that a failed login attempt produces a log entry with `event: AUTH_FAILURE` and the source IP.

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:26`: it applies **Add security audit logging**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Log authn / authz outcomes with stable correlation ids.
logger.info({ event: 'auth.login.fail', userId, ip: req.ip, reqId })
logger.info({ event: 'authz.deny', userId, route: req.path, reqId })
```

**Verification:** A failed admin probe leaves an `authz.deny` line in the central log within 1 s.

2. Remove the `secrets.DOCKERHUB_TOKEN` usage from `release.yml` docker/login-action step; `replace` with the OIDC id-token approach (add `permissions: id-token: write` at the job level and use the docker/login-action OIDC flow).
3. Add a Cosign step after docker/build-push-action to sign and attest the published image, satisfying [`SC-007`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/supply-chain-dependency-security#sc-007) provenance requirements.
4. Verify in a dry-run release job that the `token` lifetime is scoped to the workflow run and that no static credential persists in secrets.

**Verification:** Trigger a test release workflow and confirm in the Docker Hub activity log that the push was authenticated via OIDC (no long-lived `token` used); verify the published image has a Cosign attestation: `cosign verify bkimminich/juice-shop:<tag> --certificate-identity-regexp='.*' --certificate-oidc-issuer https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`.

<a id="m-042"></a>
#### M-042 — Harden the authentication flow

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-042](#f-042) — OAuth Implicit Flow Without State (`login.component.ts:148`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Requirements at stake:**

- [`AC-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-004) (MUST) - Workforce and privileged users MUST authenticate through the…
- [`WEB-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/web-frontend-security#web-002) (MUST) - Authentication tokens, secrets, and sensitive personal data MUST NOT be…

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** High · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**How:**

1. Change `googleLogin()` to use `response_type=code` with PKCE (S256 `code_challenge`) and a cryptographically random state parameter stored in `sessionStorage` before the redirect.
2. Implement a backend BFF endpoint that exchanges the authorization code for tokens, keeping the `access_token` out of the browser entirely.
3. In the OAuth callback, validate the returned state against the `sessionStorage` value before proceeding; reject mismatches with an error.
4. Write an integration test confirming the redirect URL contains state=`<random>` and `code_challenge`, and that a callback with a mismatched state is rejected.

**Verification:** Capture the OAuth redirect URL; confirm it contains `code_challenge` and state parameters. Submit a callback with a mismatched state and confirm the app rejects it.

**Blueprint:** [BP-SPA-BFF](https://appsec.int.example.com/blueprints/spa-bff) - Security Blueprint for SPAs with BFF · [Browser Storage](https://appsec.int.example.com/blueprints/spa-bff#_browser_storage) · [Use a Backend-for-Frontend (BFF)](https://appsec.int.example.com/blueprints/spa-bff#_bff) (for [`AC-004`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/authentication-access-control#ac-004), [`WEB-002`](https://appsec.int.example.com/req/web-frontend-security#web-002))

**Reference:** [datatracker.ietf.org: Rfc7636](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7636)

**How:**

2. Bind the verified wallet address to the authenticated user's session so re-use by a different session is rejected.
3. Add an integration test that submits a wallet address from one session that was minted in another and verifies the response is 401/403.

**Verification:** Submit a wallet address that appears in `addressesMinted` using a JWT for a different account than the one that performed the on-chain mint; the endpoint must return 401 or 403 without deleting the Set entry.

**Verification:** Run `grep -r '@main\|@v[0-9]' .github/workflows/` and confirm zero matches after pinning; run a CI build and verify all steps resolve from pinned SHAs in the Actions log.

2. Add pagination parameters (offset, limit) validated server-side to a maximum of 100 rows per request.

## 11. Out of Scope

### Not Covered by This Method

This is a code-derived threat model at implementation level: it is built from source, configuration and git history, models the system as built rather than as designed, and does not `replace` a design-time review.

- Design intent and the reasoning behind it - no design documents, ADRs or workshop context are read.
- Business processes and user journeys that leave the code.
- Runtime behaviour, deployment topology and production-only configuration.
- External and organizational controls.

Treat this report as review input, not sign-off.

### Excluded from This Assessment

The following items are **explicitly excluded** from this threat model. Findings against these areas should be tracked separately.

- Third-party hosted dependencies and SaaS endpoints
- Browser runtime vulnerabilities and end-user device security
- Operating system kernel and container runtime
- Underlying network infrastructure (DNS, BGP, ISP)
- Physical security of hosting facilities

<a id="appendix-a-vektor-taxonomy"></a>
## Appendix A — Vektor Taxonomy

This appendix defines the attacker-starting-position labels used in the Top Threats table and throughout [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register). Each label answers the question *what does the attacker need before the exploit begins?*

<a id="vektor-internet-anon"></a>
### Internet Anon

**Attacker position:** Unauthenticated attacker from the public internet · **Breach distance:** 1

**Preconditions:**

- Endpoint is reachable from the internet (no IP allowlist, no VPN)
- No authentication middleware blocks the request

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-user"></a>
### Internet User

**Attacker position:** Any authenticated low-privilege user (valid JWT / session) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker has signed up or otherwise obtained a valid user session
- Endpoint is behind auth but not behind role/admin checks

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A06:2025, A02:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-priv-user"></a>
### Internet Priv User

**Attacker position:** Authenticated admin-level user (JWT with admin `role` or equivalent) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker holds admin credentials or has elevated privileges
- Endpoint gated on admin `role` but still exploitable once reached

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025

<a id="vektor-victim-required"></a>
### Victim-Required

**Attacker position:** Attacker needs victim interaction - social engineering, crafted link, or live session · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Victim must click a link, load a page, or have an active session
- Applies to XSS, CSRF, click-jacking, open redirect

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [CWE-1021](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1021.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025

<a id="vektor-build-time"></a>
### Build-Time

**Attacker position:** Attacker controls a build input - CI runner, dependency, base image, or external data fetched during build · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Compromise of a dependency, registry, or base image
- OR compromise of a CI runner with write access to artifacts

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [CWE-1039](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1039.html) · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A08:2025

<a id="vektor-repo-read"></a>
### Repo-Read

**Attacker position:** Attacker gains read access to source repository (leaked clone, forked fork, insider, compromised developer workstation) · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Read access to the source tree at or after commit time
- No runtime exploit needed - the vulnerability is the content of the repo

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [CWE-540](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/540.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A04:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-n-a"></a>
### n/a

**Attacker position:** Architectural / meta-finding - no runtime entry point, the finding describes a design defect aggregating multiple code-level findings

**Preconditions:**

- Finding is AF-NNN (architectural) rather than F-NNN (code-level)
